Title: Sulpicii Severi libri qui supersunt, recensuit et commentario critico instruxit Carolus Halm (Corpus Scriptorum Ecclesiasticorum Latinorum vol. I)
Creator: Sulpicius Severus (c. 363-c. 425); Karl Halm (ed.)
Publisher: Vindobonae: apud C. Geroldi filium bibliopolam Academiae
Publication Date: 1866 (MDCCCLXVI on the title page, file line 147; Halm's preface is dated Munich, August 1866, at file line 485)
Rights: Public Domain (pd-us-by-date: published 1866); the archive.org record tags it NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT
Language: lat (Latin; Google OCR with frequent letter confusions, readable; no Greek in the volume)
Source: archive.org item sulpiciiseveril00halmgoog (digitized by Google from the Bodleian Library copy; catalogue volume 1 of the Corpus), full text at https://archive.org/download/sulpiciiseveril00halmgoog/sulpiciiseveril00halmgoog_djvu.txt. Fetched directly 2026-10-17 by the Shared-Events source-research thread (Era 2 worker A); the byte count of the downloaded file (670,600) equals the size archive.org lists for the djvu.txt.
Provenance note: Extracted unaltered: the item's own djvu.txt is reproduced whole after this header; nothing was stripped, normalized or corrected, so Google's scan notice and OCR errors remain as scanned. Rights basis: an 1866 Vienna printing is out of US copyright under this project's pd-us-by-date rule; the host's NOT_IN_COPYRIGHT tag is supporting evidence.
Content note: The Latin original behind the English Sulpitius Severus in npnf211_sulpitius-severus-vincent-lerins-cassian.xml: the Chronica (heading at file line 497), the Vita Martini, the Epistulae and the Dialogi. Chronicle book II carries the Aquitanian memory of the Arian crisis written about 403: the council of Ariminum summoned under the prefect Taurus and the Gallic and British bishops who refused the emperor's rations (file line 5388), the council of Seleucia and Hilary's exile (file line 5451), Phoebadius (file line 5582) and Hilary's return and death (file line 5664). A near-contemporary Gallic narrative one generation after the events, not an eyewitness.
